# Parity ledger — mnml-zig against mnml 0.2.21

One row per bullet of the Rust repo's `FEATURES.md` (a bullet that lists
several separable things is several rows). Every claim below was checked
by reading this worktree's source — a runner in a `pub const table`, a
`Pane` variant, a config field that something reads — not by trusting a
report. A spec id without a runner is `missing`, whatever its title says.

| status | meaning |
|---|---|
| `done` | implemented; the file names it |
| `partial` | some of it exists; the note says what is missing |
| `cut` | left out on purpose; the note says why and where the user learns it (a toast, this page) |
| `missing` | nothing found |

The cutover decision reads the totals and the Remaining list first.

The two integration sections at the bottom (jira: 44 done, 1 cut, of 45;
bitbucket: 41 done, 1 cut, of 42) are counted apart: they measure the
integrations against their Rust apps, not the editor against 0.2.21.

The first ledger (at `de423c5`) printed 278 / 36 / 10 / 149 of 473; the
same script over that file counts 279 / 36 / 10 / 149 of 474 — the old
table was tallied by hand and off by one. Three rows were added since:
the `ui.*` toggles (a Remaining item before, a row now), Lua scripting
and bridge v2 — the last two beyond the Rust list. Two more since: the
Lua decorations + diagnostics sink and the idle hook / hidden task that
the two shipped example scripts are written against, both Zig-authored.
Three more since: the live picker source with its preview column, the
list helper with its rail sections, and the text operations with
operator registration — the three shapes examples 3, 4 and 5 are
written against, all Zig-authored. Two more since: installed scripts
(a script as a directory, one Lua state each, the three tabs) and the
platform polish that closed §5 of the design — `mnml.inspect`, the
argument-error contract, the budget chip, `script.doctor`'s roots and
the doc check that keeps `docs/LUA.md` and the registry in step.

Ids: 1141 in `src/commands/specs.zig` (2026-09-23/24: `term.search` / `search_next` / `search_prev` (scrollsearch), `qf.open` / `qf.close` / `qf.from_diagnostics` and `view.search_history` / `search_history_backward` (searchfix), `find.word_forward_partial` / `word_backward_partial` (vimfix), `git.checkout_commit` (gitpolish), `sessions.changes` (sessiondiff) and `ai.claude_add_account` / `ai.claude_remove_account` (usagemeters) landed; `ai.canary` and four dead `cloud_agents.*` stubs that only toasted "not in this build" went (aifix); the four session-worktree ids — `ai.new_session_worktree`, `sessions.open_worktree_in_tree` / `merge_worktree` / `remove_worktree` — landed 2026-09-10); 1141 have runners (32 of them the deliberate `cutRunner` stubs, each naming the cut and the PARITY section that records it), none without — `view.toggle_zoom` landed with the fullscreen track (`zen.zig`) and `integrations.icon_picker` with the leftovers track. `zig build -Dpartial=false` builds, and CI runs it.

## Landed since the first ledger

The first ledger was written at `de423c5` (Phases 0–8). Ten merges since,
each with a `docs/parity-notes/<track>.md` (folded into this page and
deleted at `5498f87`) that named the rows it flipped and the file proving each; every claim was re-checked against the source
before the row moved (a disputed one is at the bottom). The notes are
folded in here and in `docs/WAVE3_CONTRACT.md`'s `// changed:` sections.

changed rows: 159 flips, 3 new rows), each row attributed to the note
that named it.

Rust's counterpart: ~245 curated entries in `info_view_copy.rs`, no audit, no lint. Corpus: `hover_help_curated`. Second pass: the `.docs` link kind — `comptime copy.docsSection("The launcher dock")` opens a docs/CONFIG.md section (embedded, `src/app/docs.zig`) as a read-only markdown preview on a virtual `mnml-docs://` path the session restores; the lint fails a heading the manual lacks; six entries carry one (the dock's pin, a launcher item, both RESTRICTED branches, the INTEGRATIONS section, the Settings surface) — 1220 targets, 708 curated. `mnml-zig hover-audit` now flushes its writer: the table ran past the 4 KB buffer and the summary and NEW-uncovered lines never printed. (2) **sev2** — a server's request with a STRING id (zls's `workspace/configuration`, `"id":"i_haz_configuration"`) was read with `getInt`, filed as a notification and never answered, so zls never resolved its `zig_lib_path` and every definition / hover / completion into std came back null and `.settings` never arrived: `jsonrpc.Id` (`int | str`), `getId`, `Kind.request.id` an `Id`, `respond` echoes it as JSON (`lsp_string_id_request.test`; confirmed against zls 0.16.0 — the wire carries the reply and `gd` on `std.testing.expectEqual` lands in `std/testing.zig`). (3) inlay hints that arrived with the reply to the request sent on OPEN never painted, and the toggle painted nothing: an untouched document's edit-log head IS 0 and the decor sets used `0 = never landed`; `Set.seq` / `Track.seq` / `hint_seq` and `SemFile.seq` are optionals now, so the first reply is fresh and code lenses / colours / links / semantic tokens are requested on the first frame too (`lsp_inlay_hints_on_open.test`). (4) a `.lsp` entry written after launch for an extension a default owns was shadowed by that default — `ensureServer` re-read the config only when nothing matched; it re-reads before the first match now, and `App.reloadConfig` (a trust change) re-arms the re-read and forgets the dead / missing servers (`lsp.configReloaded`; `lsp_config_refresh_default_ext.test`). (5) `onPath` found a server on the App's PATH and the spawn missed it — `std.process.spawn` resolves a bare argv[0] on the process's PATH, not the map it is handed; the walk's result (`runners.pathOf`) is what is spawned (`lsp_env_path_spawn.test` — a `# env: PATH` shim can stand in for a language server now). (6) `test.run_at_cursor` on a Zig project answered with four other ecosystems: `Project.zig` (`build.zig`), `Runner.zig` in the TESTS pane — `zig build test`, `zig test <file>`, `-Dtest-filter=<name>` when the build.zig declares it (this repo's does) else `--test-filter`, `parseZig` for both report shapes, the passes from the build's tally, a failure's own frame, a passed test found by its `test "…"` line, `zig` in `known_tools`, `tools/shims/zig` for the corpus (`zig_runner_test_at_cursor.test`, `zig_runner_file_filter.test`). No new ids |

The UI thread owns it: subsystems begin where they start a worker and end where its result is handled, and a worker with something of its own to say posts the new `.job` event (`jobs.post`) — the lint worker (`exit 2: …` when a tool fails with nothing parsed, `N findings` otherwise) and the integration poller (each `--values` run). Either click, `space j` or (standard) `Ctrl+K J` (uxfix) opens the JOBS overlay (`jobs.show`, `src/ui/jobs_view.zig`: a `ListPanel` in a modal box, `PanelId.jobs`): RUNNING with elapsed time and a Cancel row where the job can be stopped (test run, streamed send, search walk, a server still starting), FINISHED newest first with outcome and duration; Enter opens the job's pane or toasts its words, `c` cancels; opening it dismisses the held failure. | `regressfix` | 2026-09-24 | the regression sweep's worth-a-look list (`h26/hunt/regress/REPORT.md`), one commit each. (1) **sev1 shape** — closing a browser pane whose Chrome ignores SIGTERM (wedged on a keychain prompt under a private `HOME`) hung `App.deinit` for good in `wait4`: `core/child.zig` `terminate` sends SIGTERM, polls `waitpid(WNOHANG)` for a bounded grace (2 s), then SIGKILLs and reaps — never a `wait` after a kill — and Chrome leads its own process group so its helpers go too (`cdp.Launch.kill`, the launcher's error paths, the runner's step child). (2) SEARCH decides `git grep` with the fenced lookup (`git.repoFor`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`) and only where `git ls-files --error-unmatch .` finds tracked files: an untracked folder, an unborn repo or a fenced-off enclosing checkout go to `rg` / the walk instead of `0 hits (git grep)`. (3) a file's environment carries `SHELL` (`/bin/zsh` on macOS, the host's elsewhere); `docs/TESTING-hermetic.md` names Chrome's keychain and rustup's proxies as the two `HOME`-reading tools, with their workarounds. (4) `x` over context rows only says the selection holds no change instead of asking to discard the hunk; the `Warn.cs ✗2` after a C# rename is the fixture's own `=> Calc.` (CS1001 / CS1002), not stale. (5) the sessions table names a long session by its first prompt — uxfix's one namer; a file now holds it. `tests/e2e/search_section_untracked_repo.test`, `git_diff_discard_context_selection.test`, `sessions_table_long_session_name.test` |

## Remaining — what is still `missing` or `partial`, with an estimate

S = a day, M = a few days, L = a week or more, for one person who knows
the tree. Nothing left is larger than M.

| item | size | section |
|---|---|---|
| (none — every spec id has a runner or a `cutRunner`; `zig build -Dpartial=false` builds and CI runs it) | — | Headless, IPC & extensibility |

Everything else the first Remaining list named landed on the `remaining`
branch (2026-09-05; the corpus was 351/352 then — the one failure asserted TOML; it asserts ZON since 2026-09-07 and the corpus is 393/393): MRU buffers, pins and `tab.reopen`; the symbol,
snippet and fold commands; the NvChad which-key groups; find history;
the `⟳` chip menu with auto-refresh and the SEARCH / AGENTS row menus;
Settings → AI and the 60 % × 70 % box; the legacy launcher-script row;
the HTTP `+` chip and the per-field request menu; `Alt`-drag copies and
move-to completion; the palette bar's codicons, `+` chip, narrow rule,
stress copy and the clock; markdown glance tabs; the five unread `ui.*`
fields; the glyph audit / bake commands; workspace manifests as a
trust sink. Each row names its file and its test.

## Cuts — and where the user learns it

| cut | why | the user sees |
|---|---|---|
| Local in-process FIM model (`mnml-fim-engine`) | a bundled model is a release-size and build-time cost mnml-zig does not carry; ghost text is API-only | `ai.suggest_backend = local` toasts the migration note (`src/app/ai.zig` header) |
| brotli response decoding | `std.compress` has gzip / deflate / zstd, not brotli; the HTTP client asks for the encodings it can decode | `Accept-Encoding` never lists `br` (`src/http/client.zig`); a forced `br` body is shown raw with a toast |
| WebP images | no decoder in `zigimg` for this release; PNG / JPEG / GIF render over kitty / iTerm2 / sixel | `view.image_open` and the markdown preview show the `[image: alt]` placeholder for `.webp` |
| Glyph-builder SVG preview and Nerd Font patching | The SVG-to-font half now exists in Zig and needs no FontForge (`src/glyph/`: an SVG reader, an sfnt writer, `zig build font`) — it builds the whole `MnmlSymbols` face, and `view.terminal_glyph_custom` / `view.claude_mark_custom` rebake it from a user's SVG. What is cut is the per-integration builder pane and its live preview | `integrations.glyph_builder` / `patch_nerd_font_svg` / `edit_codex_glyph` toast the reason (`src/app/cmd_app.zig` `cutRunner`); `edit_claude_glyph` was REMOVED on 2026-09-21 — the Claude chip's menu row that fired it is now `Icon ▸ Custom SVG…`, which bakes |
| TOML anywhere (config, themes, manifests, `trusted_workspaces.toml`) | E1 / E2: every persisted format is ZON; the final Rust release ships `mnml export-config-zon` | `docs/CONFIG.md`; `settings_persist_to_workspace.test` asserts the workspace `.mnml/config.zon` (the Rust corpus asserted a TOML file there — re-aimed 2026-09-07, so the corpus reads 393/393) |
| The Rust integration binaries and the crates.io marketplace of them (`mnml-forge-*`, `mnml-aws-*`, …) | E5: integrations are rewritten in Zig on a v2 bridge after the cutover; the 0.2.x crates stay published for 0.2.x users | `pr.picker` / `pr.refresh` toast the reason; `:term <binary>` still runs any installed binary as a pty pane; a `crates_keyword` marketplace source is accepted and lists nothing |
| Sonos transport | a sibling-app IPC; not a terminal-IDE concern for the successor | every `sonos.*` / `audio.*` id toasts the reason (`cutRunner`); `sonos.*` config keys are accepted and ignored. *// changed 2026-09-07:* the now-playing chip is DONE (`src/app/now_playing.zig`: idle pair, transport, the macOS/mixr poller, `MNML_NOW_PLAYING` for tests); `mixr.set_preferred_*` / `mixr.copy_track` run, the rest of `mixr.*` still toast |
| Playwright as the generic `test.*` runner | the generic `test.*` runners keep the project's own command (cargo / npm / go / pytest); Playwright has its own ids (`test.run_playwright*`, the Tests pane) — listed here because the spec titles still say "Playwright" | `test.run_*` run the project's own test command. *2026-09-22 (`lsp-fixes`):* on a Cargo crate `test.run_file` runs the file's TARGET (`--lib` / `--bins` / `--bin x` / `--test x`) or its module-path filter (`net::http::`) — `cargo test <stem>` was a name filter that ran 0 tests and said `ok`; 0 tests matched is toasted (`runners.cargoTestArgs`, `runners.onFrame`; `tests/e2e/test_run_file_cargo_target.test`) |

| Code folding — the gutter's chevrons | done | `foldGlyph` / `signFor` / `Doc.foldable` in `src/ui/editor_view.zig`, `mark_priority.fold` / `fold_hover`, `.fold_arrow` in `src/ui/hit.zig`, `foldArrowPress` in `src/app/dispatch.zig`, `foldStartsAt` in `src/app/cmd_editor.zig` | *2026-09-20, `fold-gutter`:* a folded line always wears `▶` in the sign cell — above a diagnostic and git's bars, below a breakpoint and the debugger's arrow, as Rust's ladder has it — and a foldable line wears `▼` while the pointer is on it, last in the ladder so the offer never hides a fact. `ui.expand_indicator` picks the pair (`▸` / `▾`), `--ascii` gives `>` / `v`. Either cell is a 1-cell `.fold_arrow` hit registered over the row's `.gutter`, and a left press runs `editor.toggle_fold` on that line, so the mouse and `za` share one path. Foldable is the COMMAND's rule (`foldStartsAt` reuses `foldRangeFrom`), not Rust's trailing-`{[(:` guess — a chevron never offers a fold the command refuses. `ui.always_show_fold_arrows` wears the offer on every foldable line with no pointer at all (the key shipped in `Config.zig` unread until this track); it is `:set`-able, documented in `docs/CONFIG.md`, and a Settings → Editor row (*Always show fold arrows*). A right press on the chevron opens the gutter menu, which is what Rust does too — its `editor_gutters` rect is tested before `fold_arrows`, so Rust's own fold-arrow right-click arm is unreachable. One knowing divergence: when a breakpoint or the debugger's arrow outranks the fold on a folded line, Zig gives that cell to the debugger and registers no `.fold_arrow` there, where Rust keeps the fold's click rect and lets it take the press. `tests/e2e/fold_gutter_chevron.test` |

| Tab pages `:tab*` with independent trees | done | `src/app/cmd_tab.zig`, `Layouts` in `layout.zig` | `tab.reopen` (`tabReopen`, `App.closed_tabs`, 8 deep) — `// changed:` a closed page's clean panes are closed, so its files come back as tabs of one leaf after the current page, the active one focused; `tests/e2e/buffer_pin_reopen.test` — `:tabclose` / `:tabonly` leave a pane another page still shows to that page (`shownElsewhere`); `tab.reopen` (`tabReopen`, `App.closed_tabs`, 8 deep) — `// changed:` a closed page's clean panes are closed, so its files come back as tabs of one leaf after the current page, the active one focused; `tests/e2e/buffer_pin_reopen.test` |
| Bufferline tab strip | done | `src/ui/bufferline.zig`, per-leaf strips in `render.zig` | the ` +N hidden ` chip counts the tabs off either edge of the window plus the filtered ones (2026-09-10, Rust's `tabs.len() - painted_count`); a click opens the buffer picker; `tests/e2e/tabs_hidden_chip.test`. *2026-09-20 (hunt-fixes):* it PAINTS whenever any tab is off the strip. It used to be laid after the last chip and only if what was left before the chevrons happened to hold it — which is room only when the strip is scrolled to a tail that fits whole; in the ordinary overflow case the last chip is cut at the right edge, nothing is left, and twelve buffers at 120 columns said nothing about the missing ones. `stripRight` decides the chips' right edge before a cell is painted: where the chip fits in what is left after the last chip and the `󰐕` it takes that, so a whole tail lays out cell for cell as before; where it does not, it is GIVEN its cells off the chips' edge, because a strip that hides tabs and says nothing about them is the bug. It never takes the `󰐕`'s room. `fitActive` reads the same edge, so the scroll clamp and the paint agree. `tests/e2e/tab_strip_hidden_count.test`. *2026-09-13 (tab-icons):* a pty tab's mark is the pane's, not one generic terminal — `ptyIcon` in `render.zig`, Rust's `ui/mod.rs::pty_icon`: Claude's `\u{F1E00}` and Codex's `\u{F1E01}` (the codepoints Rust pins on the `claude_code` / `codex` integration rows) for an AI session, painted in the pane's accent — the same colour as its identity strip and its sessions card — the host terminal's own mark in white for a plain shell, and the codicon terminal for any other command; `tests/e2e/pty_tab_icons.test` |

The dock is the THIRD host in the section-placement model, not a separate widget — `body = [left col \| splits \| right col]` over `[dock]`, carved off `upper` before the columns as Rust's is. `ui.bottom_panel_height` (12 rows, clamped 3..60) and `ui.bottom_panel_visible`; a one-row divider above it drags to resize, and `Ctrl-W +` / `-` resize it from the keys (the same chords, which now also resize a focused column by `>` / `<` — neither had a keyboard path before). Two thirds of `upper` is the cap and the body keeps two rows, so the dock never takes the editor's last row; under six rows of `upper` none is carved. Rows ride in `session.zon` (`bottom` / `bottom_panel_height`) beside the columns' |

It now lands in the dock. `ui.section_side.diagnostics = .right` in the config keeps the old placement |
| Host a pane in the bottom panel — `view.host_active_in_bottom_panel` | done | `host` / `unhost` / `drain` in `src/app/bottom.zig`, `drawBottomDock` / `drawPaneContent` in `render.zig`; `tests/e2e/bottom_dock_host_pane.test` | *2026-09-14 (bottom-dock):* was a `cutRunner`. The active pane leaves the split tree and lives in the dock (kept in `App.panes`, out of the layout — `outline_panel`'s shape), behind its own tab strip: focus, close, middle-click and the tab menu all work as on a leaf. *// changed:* running the id again on a docked pane sends it BACK to the splits, which Rust has no door for; the toggle drains every hosted pane back rather than dropping it, as Rust's does. Rust refuses editor / pty / request kinds ("not hostable yet") — here every pane kind draws, since the dock calls the same per-kind painter a leaf does |
| Move a section to the bottom dock — `Ctrl-W J` / `K`, the rail menu, `:sidebar bottom`; `Ctrl-W j` / `k` focus it | done | `ctrlWSectionSide` / `came_from` in `src/app/side.zig`, the move rows in `src/app/context_menus.zig` | Zig-only (Rust has no section placement at all). `J` docks the focused section, `K` returns it to the column it came from; the rail's right-click grows a third *Move to bottom dock* row, and `:sidebar bottom` is the word form. Lowercase `Ctrl-W j` / `k` are the focus step — the dock is a window under the panes, the tree and both columns, so `j` reaches it from any of them (`focusDir` in `cmd_view.zig`). No new command ids — the spec count stays 1068 |
| Session — panes, layout, tab pages, chrome, pins, history | done | `src/app/session.zig` (`.mnml/session.zon`, `session.save` / `restore` / `clear`, `terminalRestore`), `PtyPane.dormant` + `OpenOptions.dormant` in `src/app/pty_pane.zig` | Zig-only; a stale / foreign file is one toast; dock widgets ride along; // changed (bottom-dock): the dock's section and height ride too. *2026-09-20 (restore-running), on the user's word — this replaces the same day's dormant-by-default rule, which they hit on their first restart and asked "why is this necessary?":* a restart hands the terminals back **working**. A plain shell pane comes back RUNNING, a fresh one in the cwd it was saved in; a Claude Code pane whose session id was saved comes back RESUMING it (`claude --resume <id>` — never a second session under an existing id, and never a new billed one, so a missing id falls to the next rule); anything else — an arbitrary command line, a bare `claude` — comes back **dormant**, its tab, its title and `[exited] — any key restarts <label>`, where a key runs the `term.restart` that already existed. `session.restore_terminals = .running \| .dormant` (default `.running`) is the one switch, with a Settings row beside *Restore session on open*. *2026-09-21 (codex-resume):* a **Codex** pane is in rule 2 now too — `codex resume <id>` (`cli.codexResumeArgv`, which keeps the pane's own binary and the options `resume` accepts and drops its positionals, so a stale prompt is never re-sent). Codex takes no session id when it starts one, so the id is DISCOVERED rather than read off the argv: `src/ai/codex_rollout.zig` walks `~/.codex/sessions/**/rollout-*.jsonl` and keeps the one whose `session_meta` names the pane's cwd and dates the session at or after `PtyPane.started_at_s` (sampled before the spawn) — and only when EXACTLY one answers, which `session.zig`'s `paneSessionId` then remembers on the pane and writes into `Saved.Pane.session_id`. Two candidates is none: `codex resume --last` is never the fallback, because the newest session on the machine is not necessarily this pane's, and two Codex panes opened back-to-back in one directory can only ever identify the younger one. `tests/e2e/session_restore_terminal_running.test`, `session_restore_claude_resume.test`, `session_restore_codex_resume.test`, `session_restore_command_pane_dormant.test`, `session_restore_terminal_dormant.test`. *2026-09-21 (session-kinds), off the "moving my whole day in" hunt:* the file kept editors, previews and terminals, so **a review in progress was lost on a restart** — a git status pane and a workspace Search pane were not session kinds at all. Five query-shaped kinds joined `Saved.PaneKind`: `git_status`, `grep`, `git_graph`, `diff` and `image`. Every one of them writes down its **subject**, never its answer — a repo's absolute root (`Repo.id` is per-process), a search query plus its case / whole-word / regex options and the row the cursor was on, a diff's scope — and comes back by RE-RUNNING that query (`git.repoByPath` discovers the repos on the way in; `grep.restorePane` re-runs the search and puts the cursor back when the run says it is done, since the hits arrive batch by batch long after the pane opens). A subject that is gone is skipped **quietly**: the directory is not a repo any more, the diffed file was deleted, the image went — no pane, no toast, and never an empty shell. Not saved, and why: a diff of a `.commit` / `.range` / `.orig` / `.conflict` (reachability cannot be checked without running git, and a diff pane that opens onto an error is worse than one that does not come back), an `outline` (it names another pane's id and needs a live LSP), a `list` pane — quickfix / location / stash files / git log (its rows ARE the result; there is no query to re-run), and the HTTP / websocket / browser / debug / AI panes (a response and a debug session are not re-runnable). The TODOS / NOTES / FINDINGS / SESSIONS panels are sections, not panes, and already rode in `left` / `right` / `bottom`. Backward compatible both ways: an older file has none of the new fields and gets their defaults; a newer FIELD is ignored (`ignore_unknown_fields`). A newer *kind* is the one-way part — an older build cannot parse the enum literal and ignores the whole file with the existing "does not parse" toast, which is a toast, not a crash. `tests/e2e/session_restore_git_status_grep.test`; unit round-trips + the subject-gone and old/new-field-set cases in `src/app/session.zig` |

Only a leading prefix counts, so `src/a>b.txt` stays a path. `>` is the non-kitty route to the palette: `palette` is `ctrl+shift+p` alone, and a terminal without the kitty keyboard protocol delivers that as `ctrl+p` — `cmd_picker.quickOpenPrefix`, `tests/e2e/quickopen_prefixes.test` |

A **deliberate departure**: the standard profile has no leader to press, its popup opens on `Ctrl+K` (`docs/KEYMAP_PROFILES.md` rule 2), and the Info panel on the same screen already says `Ctrl+K Ctrl+I` — a header naming a key the profile does not carry sent VS Code users looking for it. `steps-whichkey` gains the header row (14 → 15 differing lines beyond the rail); the vim profile's screen is untouched. `tests/e2e/whichkey_standard_title.test`. *2026-09-14:* the root's `r → +lsp` (NvChad's `<leader>ra`, which the reference popup does not list) is `vim_only`, as `+debug` already was — `steps-whichkey` went from 6 rows differing beyond the rail to 2, and the popup's fifteen rows now match the reference cell for cell. `<BS>` climbs a level, a non-character key leaves the popup open (the reference ignores it; this one used to close), a dead end toasts `no leader mapping: <leader>…` (`Ctrl+K …` in the standard profile since 2026-09-20), and every row is a click target (the popup sorts, so a row carries the caller's index). *2026-09-14 (the user's call — do it the reference plugin's way):* the popup now carries **per-group icons and per-group chord counts**, which the reference editor's popup (`rust-whichkey-120x40.txt`) has neither of — a **deliberate departure**, so `steps-whichkey` goes from 2 rows differing beyond the rail to 14 and stays there — the twelve new ones are the popup's twelve content rows; the header, the `esc to cancel` hint and the box are untouched, and the other two predate this row (a tree marker and the workspace pill). A group row reads `󰍉 f → +find (7)`, a leaf wears its group's face dimmed (`󰘵 q → close buffer` at the root), and the header of a level inside a group is that group's own row — `┌ <leader>f  +find (7) `. The count is `whichkey.chordCount`, a walk of the tree, so a chord added below moves the number with nothing else to edit; the table is `src/ui/whichkey_glyph.zig` and it **takes** the faces rather than re-picking them — the rail's own `activity_bar.Section.meta()` for find / git / debug / integrations / http / buffers, `icons.zig`'s devicons for the `+cargo` / `+npm` / `+pytest` / `+go` runners, the menu glyphs for the verbs — so the popup and the rest of the chrome cannot drift. Each has its `--ascii` twin (one cell either way, so the strip's column math does not move), and a group the table does not know gets the neutral `󰘵`. `tests/e2e/whichkey_icons.test`; the unit test in `app/whichkey.zig` fails if a group has no face or a count stops matching a walk of the keymap |

The cursor follows its LINE through a whole-document replace (`mapCursor`: the line whose non-blank characters are the cursor line's — or begin it, when the formatter split it — nearest the old line number, at the same count of non-blank characters in; a line with no twin keeps its line and column) — the byte offset was kept as it was, so every line ruff added above the cursor pushed it onto an earlier, unrelated line (`def total(self)` landed on `self.b = b`) |

A discard now drops the worktree change relative to the index and the index is untouched; a fully staged file reads `(no changes)` rather than the whole file as an addition (the untracked fallback fires only for a file `ls-files` does not know); `git_diff_file_index_base.test`, `git_diff_file_discard_staged_line.test`. *Same day (`git-diff-binary-says-no-changes.md`), Zig-only — Rust's pane is empty for a binary file:* a binary file's pane read `(no changes)`, the clean file's text; it reads `Binary file changed (33 → 40 bytes)` now, the sizes off one `--stat` call made only when the diff names a binary file (`parse.binarySizes`); `git_diff_binary.test` |

Any other failure carries git's `error:` / `fatal:` line (`Out.failLine`) rather than a `hint:`; the error toast keeps its four seconds and the reason is cut to the box's four rows, not one, so the half of the sentence that says what to do is not the half that is lost; `git_push_rejected_toast.test`, `git_pull_diverged_toast.test` |

Two or more repos: each takes a palette slot in discovery order the first time it is seen, written to the home config's `git.repo_colors` (the first assignment wins across restarts); right-click on the repo pill lists `Color: …` per palette entry with the one in effect ticked and `Color: Auto` last (Auto ticked only once chosen; under All repos the right-click stays the repos menu); the pill's column 0, each All-repos sub-header's gutter, a one-cell `▌` down the left edge of the repo's graph / status / diff panes, their tab glyphs and the tree's repo dot paint it; one repo paints nothing |
| Pane rails — every pane a colour off one ladder | done (Zig-authored) | `src/ui/pane_rail.zig`, `src/app/pane_accent.zig`, `PaneStore.accents` / `assignAccent` / `Pane.wearsOwnAccent` in `src/app/pane.zig`, `pane_accent.paintsOwnStripe`, `accent_color.firstFree`, `Config.Ui.pane_rail`; `tests/e2e/pane_rail_terminals.test` / `pane_rail_editor.test` / `pane_rail_off.test` | Zig-authored — the Rust editor paints the identity strip on a Claude session pane and on a repo's git panes, and nothing else. Here every pane wears a one-cell `▌` down its left edge in the colour it took when it opened: the first slot off the shared accent ladder (`ui/accent_color.zig`, the one the SESSIONS panel draws from) that no live pane is wearing, kept for the pane's life and given back when it closes, so two terminals side by side are never the same colour and a closed pane's colour is the next pane's. `PaneStore.add` hands it out, so no caller has to ask. A pane that already belongs to something wears that owner's colour rather than a slot: a git status / diff / graph pane its repo's accent when the workspace has more than one repo to tell apart. Two kinds paint that first column themselves and get no rail over it (`pane_accent.paintsOwnStripe`): a mounted integration, whose sibling owns every cell of that grid, and a multi-repo git pane, whose `git_palette.repoGutter` already puts the same `▌` in the same cell — painting both would be the colour twice and the content two columns in. A terminal's grid owns its first column, so its body starts one cell in, exactly as a session pane's always has; an editor's gutter opens with the sign column, which is blank on almost every line, so the rail goes in that cell and nothing on screen moves (a sign still wins the cell — an alarm is never hidden by decoration). `ui.pane_rail` is `all` (default) / `sessions` (the older look) / `off`, with a Settings → UI row. *// changed 2026-09-22 (accent-defaults, the user's call):* the FIRST pane of a kind opens in a colour of its own before the ladder — a plain terminal in `white` (the theme's text colour), a Claude session in `claude_orange` (`ui/brand.zig`, the one colour the chip and the tab mark already paint), a Codex session in the `cyan` its chip wears — and every further pane of the kind takes the next free ladder slot as before (`pane_accent.defaultFor` / `kindOfArgv`, `Config.AccentDefaults` as `ui.accent_defaults` with three Settings → UI rows). "First" is by the live set: while a pane of the kind holds the default the next goes to the ladder; once it closes the default is free again. A pick from the tab's or the session card's Color menu — which now list White and Claude orange after the ladder (`accent_color.named`) — wins and rides in the session file as before; a restored pane with no remembered colour goes through the rule. `tests/e2e/pane_accent_defaults.test`, `sessions_second_claude_color.test`. Rust has no such rule: its first session is green. |

Rust's tabs read the session's name too (`rust-sessions-120x40.txt` row 1: `󱸀 fix the failing t…`, `󱸀 rel…`); the first-prompt step is Zig's, for a session whose child has set no title yet |
| Session worktrees — a session in a git worktree of its own | done (Zig-authored) | `src/app/session_worktree.zig` (`validName`, `rootFor`, `create` / `merge` / `remove`, `openNamePrompt` / `acceptName`, `confirmMerge` / `confirmRemove`), `Registry` on `sessions.State.worktrees`, `worktreeOf` / `announceWorktreeEnded` in `src/sessions.zig`, `sessionAccent` in `src/app/git_palette.zig`, `Config.LaunchProfile.worktree`, `Config.Ai.default_worktree_root`; `tests/e2e/sessions_worktree_launch_merge.test` | Zig-authored — the Rust editor has no such thing. Opt-in and off by default: *New session in a worktree…* on the AI chip's right-click (the default profile), the SESSIONS rail `+` menu, the `+` tab menu's AI rows, `+ New session` (section, table, the table's group row) and `ai.new_session_worktree`; a profile with `.worktree = true` always goes this way. The prompt is seeded `session-<n>` / `<profile>-<n>` (the first free directory) and validated as a branch name; `git worktree add -b <name> <repo>-worktrees/<name> HEAD` (`ai.default_worktree_root` overrides the root: `~` expands, a relative path sits under the repo), refused with the reason when the directory or the branch exists; the session's cwd is the tree, `MNML_WORKSPACE` names it, the tab reads `claude @ <name>`. The registry (`session.zon` `sessions_worktrees`) pairs the tree with its session by path, then by the id the scan lists; the card paints ` ⑂ <name>` after the label, the table row a muted one (`wt:<name>` in ASCII); the row menu offers *Open worktree in tree* (an extra workspace root, the repo switched to), *Merge into <branch>…* (`--no-ff`; refused while the main tree has uncommitted changes; a failed merge opens the command log) and *Remove worktree…* (`worktree remove` + `branch -d`; an unmerged branch asks once more with Force); the git panel's WORKTREES row paints the session's `▌` and carries the same two verbs; a session whose row goes ended with its tree still there toasts once — `session <name> ended — its worktree <wt> has N commits: merge / remove / keep (row menu)`. Every git child of these verbs runs synchronously and lands in the command log |

| Search the scrollback | done (Zig-authored) | `src/app/pty_search.zig` (`term.search` / `term.search_next` / `term.search_prev`), `Mark` in `src/ui/pty_view.zig`, the find bar component `src/ui/find_bar.zig` | *2026-09-22 (`scrollsearch`):* the Rust editor has no terminal search. The editor's find bar, same look and keys, lies over the pane's last row (the child keeps its size) and searches everything the terminal still holds — scrollback and screen — one logical line at a time (soft wraps joined), literal with smart case or a regex through `src/regex/` in the profile's dialect (`cmd_find.dialectFor`); `match k/N`; the matches on the `match` role, the current one on `current_match`, the view scrolled to it. vim: `/` in terminal-normal searches down from the cursor's line and `?` up, Enter lands and closes, `n` repeats the direction and `N` reverses it, a wrap says `search hit BOTTOM, continuing at TOP`; standard: `Ctrl+F` (the `find.find` chord), VS Code's terminal find — the search starts at the newest match, Enter steps up to older output and Shift+Enter down (uxfix, 2026-09-24; `term_search_direction.test`). Each step replaces the one `match k/N` toast. Closing leaves the terminal's selection on the current match, so the terminal's copy takes it. Output that lands during a search is searched too: only the active area is rescanned, the oldest rows dropping shifts the matches with them (a tracked pin tells by how much), the current match is kept when it still exists. A scan does at most 4000 rows a tick. Unit tests `scrollback search …` in `pty_search.zig`; `tests/e2e/pty_scrollback_search.test` |
| Suspend hint | done | `editor.suspend_hint` in `cmd_app.zig` | |

## Debugging (DAP)

The one deliberate departure from same-look: the Rust debug pane was
never driven by anyone, so the Zig screens are the spec
(`docs/ui-spec/zig-debug-*.txt`, cut by `tools/zig-spec.sh`), and every
row below is tested against `mnml-fake-dap` rather than a hand-written
reply. Seventeen `dap_session_*.test` and eleven `debug_*.test`
scripts. *2026-09-22 (dapfix):* the hunt against real lldb-dap and
debugpy found nine faults the fake had been permissive enough to hide;
each row below that changed says so, and the fake now refuses what the
real adapters refuse (`arguments: []`, `initialized` before `launch`).

| feature | status | Zig file(s) | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Launch | done | `src/dap/client.zig`, `src/app/dap.zig`, `dap.run` (palette) / `dap.continue` (`F5`, starts a session when there is none) | `$NAME` / `${NAME}` in an adapter's `cmd` or an argument expands from the environment; `dap.run` re-reads the config's `.dap` table when the file has no adapter yet (trusted workspaces only). *2026-09-22 (dapfix):* `launch` / `attach` goes out on the `initialize` reply and the configuration step (breakpoints, filters, `configurationDone`) waits for that reply AND `initialized`, in either order — lldb-dap and debugpy emit `initialized` only while handling `launch`, netcoredbg before its reply; an argument-less request writes `arguments: {}` (debugpy refused the `[]` an empty tuple serialised to). Both confirmed against the real adapters; `dap_session_launch_order.test`, `docs/CONFIG.md` → `.dap` has the lldb-dap / debugpy launch and attach shapes |
| Attach | done | `dap.attach` | `.request = "attach"` in `.launch` with the adapter's keys (a `pid`, debugpy's `listen`). *2026-09-22 (dapfix):* Stop on an attached session detaches — no `terminate`, `disconnect { terminateDebuggee: false }`, and the adapter gets 500 ms to act on it before it is killed (`Transport.shutdownWithin`); the toast says the process keeps running. `dap_session_attach_stop.test` reads the fake's ledger |
| Breakpoints — toggle / list / clear | done | `dap.toggle_breakpoint` (`F9` / `<leader>db`), `dap.list_breakpoints`, `dap.clear_all_breakpoints`; `types.Breakpoint` in `src/dap/types.zig` | `enabled` / `log_message` / `verified` per breakpoint; the reply's `verified` lands per file |
| Conditional breakpoints | done | `dap.toggle_breakpoint_conditional` (`Shift+F9` / `<leader>dB`) | |

| The two branded icons each have one alternate — right-click to pick | done | `Mark` / `claudeMark` / `terminalMark` + `spark_glyph` in `src/ui/bufferline.zig`, `src/app/claude_mark.zig`, `mark` / `setMark` / `rowLabel` in `src/app/terminal_glyph.zig`, `ui.claude_mark` + `ui.terminal_glyph` in `src/config/Config.zig`, `MenuAction.set_claude_mark` / `set_terminal_mark` + their arms in `src/ui/menu_glyph.zig`, `claudeIconRows` / `terminalIconRows` in `src/app/context_menus.zig`; `tests/e2e/claude_mark_menu.test`, `terminal_icon_menu.test`, `mark_settings_persist.test` | *2026-09-20, Zig-only — Rust pins one codepoint per product in `config.rs` and rewrites any other value on load.* Two choices per icon and no glyph picker: Claude wears the Claude Code figure (U+F1E00, the default) or the Anthropic spark (U+F1E02); a terminal wears Ghostty's ghost (U+F2000, the default) or the plain codicon. Each chip's right-click menu grows one `Icon ▸` group with the two rows and a tick on the live one — the Claude chip's and a Claude pty tab's, the terminal chip's and a shell tab's — and the Settings overlay carries both as discrete rows (*Claude icon*, *Terminal icon*). **A row's own glyph is the icon that row picks** (`menu_glyph.forItem` resolves `set_claude_mark` / `set_terminal_mark` through `bufferline`), so you choose by the picture and the label (*Claude Code* / *Anthropic*, *Ghostty* / *Terminal*) only says whose it is; both rows drew one generic paint brush until 2026-09-20. The point is the resolver: `bufferline.claudeMark` / `terminalMark` are the only place a tag becomes a codepoint, and `app/claude_mark.mark` / `terminal_glyph.mark` the only place the config is read, so the cluster chip, a pty tab, the statusline meter, the launcher dock and a SESSIONS card all change in the same frame. A structural test embeds each painter's source and fails if one names `\u{F1E00}` itself. `--ascii` twins stay (`✳` for the figure, `*` for the spark) and the menu rows paint them too. The config KEYS keep the older `mark` / `glyph` spelling for compatibility; *icon* is the user's word. Both rows are `MenuAction` set-rows: **no new command ids — the spec count is unchanged**. *2026-09-21 (`claude-icon`):* each `Icon ▸` group now ends in a `Custom SVG…` row — a command, not a set-row, because it prompts for a path and bakes; `ui.claude_mark` and `ui.terminal_glyph` each have three values, and the two bakes are one shared flow (`src/app/mark_bake.zig`) |
| Powerline statusline | done | `src/ui/statusline.zig` (the two lanes, the arrows at every colour hand-off), `src/app/statusline.zig` (the chips in Rust's order) | left: mode · host segments · branch · PR · file · diagnostics · symbol · macro · find; right: host segments · tests · Claude · Codex · coverage · transfer · LSP · RESTRICTED · WRAP · autosave · size · Ln/Col · Sel · stress · bell · clock · workspace · language; the row is pinned against `rust-120x40.txt` / `rust-80x24.txt`. *2026-09-22 (sh-hunt):* the ` › name ` chip is the INNERMOST container — function, method, class, module, … — whose full range holds the caret's line (`enclosingSymbol`; `Symbol.end_line` / `isContainer` in `src/lsp/types.zig`), and nothing past the last closing brace; it was the last symbol that STARTED before the caret, so inside any function that declares a `local` it named the local (`sh-lsp-breadcrumb-wrong`). A server that gives one line per symbol keeps the old rule, kept to containers. The language chip reads the detected language (`sh` for a `bin/run-all` under a bash shebang, `make` for a Makefile — both were `—`), and its click says which rule named it. `tests/e2e/lsp_breadcrumb_innermost.test` against the fake's `--symbols rich` |
| Cmdline bar | done | `render.zig` `FrameRects.cmdline` | |

Two or more candidates show, one is no popup (Tab still completes it); up to eight rows, then `(N more — Tab to cycle)`; the selection on `bg3` bold; Tab / Down write the next row into the line, Shift+Tab / Up the previous, Enter runs the line, a click on a row writes it, typing re-filters; Esc over the typed text puts the popup away first and the line second, and on a written candidate closes the line as vim's does (one Esc still bails out after a Tab). One ring (`App.cmd_complete`) serves the popup, Tab and the arrows, so they cannot drift apart. Not ported: Rust's title / key-hint columns, the `★` recent marks and the recent-commands list on an empty line |

| Launcher dock — integrations, terminals, launchers and pinned commands on one edge | done | `src/app/launcher_dock.zig`, `src/ui/launcher_dock_view.zig`, `ui.dock` (`mode` / `edge` / `placement` / `labels` / `align` / `plus` / `plus_at` / `running_mark` / `order` / `pins` / `reveal_ms` / `hide_ms`), `view.dock_toggle` / `dock_pin` / `dock_cycle_mode` / `dock_move` / `focus_dock` / `dock_unpin_item` / `dock_item_move_{prev,next,first,last}`, `integrations.pin_to_dock` / `unpin_from_dock` / `setDockOrder`, `:dock …` in `ex.zig`, `Saved.launcher_dock_pinned` in `session.zig`; `tests/e2e/launcher_dock_{always,auto_reveal,move_edge,bottom_panel,keyboard,settings,icons,align,plus,plus_right,order}.test` | *2026-09-19, Zig-only — Rust has no such surface.* macOS's Dock: the enabled integration chips, then a *New terminal* item and one row per open pty (a click focuses it), then the installed launchers that declared no chip, then `ui.dock.pins`. Items carry the integration's category colour like a pinned rail icon, and a running one wears macOS's dot *(until dock-polish, below: brightness now)*. `always` carves the strip out of the frame as the OUTERMOST band (a bottom panel then sits inside it); `auto_hide` reveals it as PAINT over the editor after `reveal_ms` in the edge band and hides `hide_ms` after the pointer leaves — no relayout, no pty resize, the `sidebar_auto` rule; `hidden` never draws, and `view.dock_toggle` is still the keyboard's one-shot door. **The outer-band rule** (`app/hover_zones.zig`, `Id.launcher_dock` at `prio_dock`): a side dock owns the outermost column outright and pushes an auto-hiding side column's reveal edge one cell in, so both stay summonable; there is no `.top` edge, because that row is the menu bar's. `ui.dock.labels` is the bottom strip's THREE forms: `.icon_label` (the default, ` glyph label `), `.icon`, the glyph alone in the same three cells a side dock uses — padding, glyph, padding, the running dot in that padding cell — with the name in the tooltip, exactly as a side dock does it, and `.label`, the word with no glyph anywhere, the dot sharing that one padding cell so the run never shuffles when a thing opens; `:dock icons` / `:dock labels` / `:dock text`, the *Show:* rows on the strip's right-click menu (`MenuAction.set_dock_labels`, no new command id), and a settings row. A side edge ignores the key, `.label` included: three cells is all it has. *// changed (dock-v2), the user's "dock gets icon mode, and icon+label mode and label only too… also orientation with center as default… also the plus icon should be here too":* `ui.dock.align` (`.start` / `.center` / `.end`, **`.center` by default** — macOS's Dock, centred) slides the run whole, on a bottom edge along the row and on a side edge down the column; which items are on the strip is measured before the slide, so moving the run can never change what is on it, and a run with no room to move reads from the start rather than off the left edge. The pin chip keeps the far end whatever it says. `align` is a Zig keyword, so the field and the file key wear the quotes the name does not (`.@"align" = .center`). `ui.dock.plus` puts the tab bar's own `+` at the head of the run — the same glyph and the same *Create…* menu, `context_menus.openNewTabMenu`, reused rather than forked; it opens where the click landed (`activateAt`), reads `+ New` under `.label`, and its tip is the verb. Five settings rows (*Launcher dock*, *… edge*, *… labels*, *… alignment*, *… + button*); `:dock start|center|end` and `:dock plus` are the word forms; the session pin rides in `session.zon`, the mode does not. Not the bottom panel (`ui.bottom_panel_*`) and not the dock widgets — eight new command ids, so the spec count moves 1091 → 1099. *2026-09-19 (edge-grip):* the BOTTOM band is the SCREEN's last row now — the `:` line's — rather than the editor area's last row two rows in: the dock is the frame's outermost edge, so an `always` strip is carved from that row (the `:` line, the statusline and the bottom panel all move up one) and a revealed one paints over it, after `drawCmdline`, so its hits win those cells. The `:` line owns the row outright: while one is open the band is not registered at all (`cmdlineBlocks`), which refuses the reveal, puts a revealed strip away AND holds the dwell clock down, so closing the line asks for a fresh `reveal_ms` instead of popping the strip up the same frame. *2026-09-20 (dock-placement), the user's "there is an extra bar at bottom of mnml zig, its below the command bar … I'd like it above the statusline I think, or maybe user picks where it goes":* both. `ui.dock.placement` is `.inner` | `.outer` and **`.inner` is the default** — the strip is the EDITOR AREA's last row again, above the statusline, so neither it nor the `:` line moves and the frame has no extra bar under the command line. `.outer` keeps the edge-grip pass's shape for whoever wants it. What did NOT move is the reveal band: it is the SCREEN's last row in both, because the edge is where a hand goes to summon a thing, so the `⋯` grip stays on the frame's own bottom row and a click there still pins. The consequences follow from the split — `cmdlineBlocks` (the reveal refusal) is `.outer`'s alone, since an `.inner` strip covers no part of the `:` line's row, while the new `gripBlocked` puts the GRIP away under both, since the grip is on that row either way. Carved and revealed are the same row in each placement, so the strip never moves when the mode does (`innerRow`, read off the bare frame, is the one place that row is computed). `:dock inner|outer` (also `above` / `below`), a *Launcher dock placement* settings row worded *above statusline* / *below command line*, two *Place:* rows on the strip's right-click menu (`MenuAction.set_dock_placement`), persisted to the home config like the mode and the edge. **No new command ids — the spec count is unchanged.** `tests/e2e/launcher_dock_placement.test`, `launcher_dock_always_outer.test`, `edge_grip_dock_outer.test`; `docs/ui-spec/zig-launcher-dock-outer-120x40.txt`  *2026-09-21 (side-band), from three `mouse` findings and one `daily` finding of the same day (`hunt/findings-2026-09-21/mouse-side-edge-grip-eats-{scrollbar,activity-bar}.md`, `mouse-sidebar-grip-overwrites-dock-items.md`, `daily-dock-left-eats-activity-bar.md`):* **the outer-band rule made real.** A SIDE dock's band is its own three columns and the frame RESERVES it for as long as the dock is not `hidden` — `auto_hide` included (`launcher_dock.banded`, `render.chrome`). Everything else is laid out inside it, so the strip stands in the same columns down, revealed and pinned: revealing relayouts nothing and covers nothing, where it used to paint straight over the activity bar and delete it — and which of the two survived depended on the order `:dock left` and `view.dock_toggle` ran in. The cost is the honest one: three columns while the strip is down, which is what the band was always documented to own. The BOTTOM edge is unchanged — a row of a twenty-four-row frame is too dear to hold empty, so only `always` is carved there and the `:` line's rule (`gripBlocked`) governs the rest. `:dock left|right|bottom` now MOVES a revealed strip instead of dismissing it, so the pair of settings reads the same in either order. `tests/e2e/launcher_dock_side_rail_order.test` *2026-09-22 (dock-polish), the user's four asks off the real bottom strip (`+  🗑 🗑 🌐 ●jira ●jira ●jira 👻`):* **(1) the `+` ends the run** — `ui.dock.plus_at = .right` (the default; `.left` is the old head), `:dock plus left|right`, a Settings row, two *+ at the … end* rows on the strip's menu worded for the edge. It is placed, never ranked: `ui.dock.order` cannot move it. **(2) the running mark is brightness** — `ui.dock.running_mark = .bright` (default) lifts the running item's glyph and word out of the `dim` the idle ones wear, the tab bar's own active/inactive rule, and spends no cell; the large `●` — the dirty dot's glyph — read as a second icon. `.dot` brings a SMALL `•` back in the ITEM's colour (never a green of its own) in the padding cell every form already keeps, so the row never shuffles; `.none` marks nothing. `:dock mark …`, a Settings row, three *Running mark:* menu rows (`MenuAction.set_dock_running_mark` / `set_dock_plus_at`). **(3) one ghost, one colour** — the dock gave its terminal items `"green"` while the split cluster's chip and a shell tab painted palette index 15; all three now read `bufferline.terminal_chip_fg`. The model's `Item.color` became a union — a ROLE for an integration (`paletteColor`, the resolver the tab cluster's chips use, so the dock's Jira is the tab's Jira) or a FIXED colour — and `launcher_dock_view.Item.color` takes the resolved `vaxis.Color`. **(4) reordering** — *Move left / right / to start / to end* on an item's menu (*up / down* on a side strip, computed from the edge), `Alt+←` / `→` / `Home` / `End` while the item has the cursor (plain `Home` / `End` jump), four command ids `view.dock_item_move_{prev,next,first,last}` (the spec count moves 1109 → 1113). `moveItem` writes the WHOLE strip's ids to `ui.dock.order` (`integrations.setDockOrder`, owned like the pins): listed ids lead, unlisted follow in default order (so a later install lands after), unknown ids are ignored, the open ptys (all `term`) move as one, the cursor follows the item. `expect color … index <N>` joined the `.test` grammar, since index 15 is not an rgb. `tests/e2e/launcher_dock_order.test`, `launcher_dock_plus_right.test`; the colour checks in `launcher_dock_always.test` |
| Pin an auto-hiding surface — `view.menu_bar_pin` and the shared 󰐃 chip | done | `State.pinned` / `mode` / `pinShown` / `togglePin` in `src/app/menu_bar.zig`, `Props.pin` + `Ids.pin` in `src/ui/menu_bar.zig`, `src/ui/pin_chip.zig` (the glyph, the hover rule, the paint), `Button.menu_bar_pin`, `openMenuBarPinMenu` in `src/app/context_menus.zig`; `tests/e2e/menu_bar_pin.test` | *2026-09-19, Zig-only — Rust has no auto-hiding chrome to pin.* The user's "the pin is a nice touch to the dock and slide ins": the menu bar now ends the same game the side columns and the launcher dock do. `view.menu_bar_pin` sets a session flag; `menu_bar.mode` reads `.always` while it is set and `ui.menu_bar` again once it is not, the shape `sidebar_auto.mode` already had. **Nothing is persisted** — the sidebar's rule, not the dock's (`Saved.launcher_dock_pinned` rides in `session.zon`): unpinning is meant to be one click. The chip is the family's now: the glyph, the `--ascii` twin `P` and the hover rule were declared and painted twice (`ui/sidebar_overlay.zig`, `ui/launcher_dock_view.zig`) and are one module, so all three pins are one piece of code. It paints at the right end of the bar's own run — past the last word, past the ` » ` — and ONLY while `ui.menu_bar` can hide the bar, so the shipped `.always` default's chrome row is cell for cell what it was. One new command id: the spec count moves 1099 → 1100 |
| Edge grip on an auto-hiding surface — the `⋯` / `⋮` handle | done | `src/ui/edge_grip.zig` (the glyphs, `place`, the paint), `drawEdgeGrips` + `Button.edge_grip_*` in `src/app/render.zig`, `gripShown` in `src/app/menu_bar.zig` / `sidebar_auto.zig` / `launcher_dock.zig`, `gripPin` in `sidebar_auto.zig`, `wordsRun` in `src/ui/menu_bar.zig`, `ui.edge_grips`; `tests/e2e/edge_grip_{menu_bar,sidebar,dock}.test` | *2026-09-19, Zig-only — Rust has no auto-hiding chrome.* The user's "can we do like ghostty does at top center, here with the 3 dots": every slide-in's edge was an invisible band that looked like editor and behaved like chrome, findable only by having been told about it. `⋯` (U+22EF) marks a row, `⋮` (U+22EE) a column, three cells at the MIDDLE of the very band `hover_zones` watches — the grip names the zone's own cell and is never a second way in, so a dwell on it reveals because it is *inside* the zone. The glyph already draws three dots in one cell, so it takes the middle cell with ground either side (the pin chip's shape); `--ascii` spends one `.` per cell. The colour is the family's — dim in the comment colour cold, full foreground on a `bg2` ground under the pointer. A left click reveals AND pins through each surface's existing pin command (**no new command id** — the count stays 1100); a right click opens that surface's own menu. A surface wears exactly one of grip and chip at a time, and neither under `.always` (nothing to summon) or `.hidden` (no zone is registered, so the handle would do nothing). The menu bar's grip sits on the run its WORDS take, not the row's middle — that is the workspace chip's, and the chip never hides. `ui.edge_grips = false` turns all three off; the bands never move, so every reveal still works. *2026-09-21 (side-band):* on a SIDE edge the run is three ROWS of one column and nothing checked what already owned them — a right-edge dock's grip owned three rows of the editor's scrollbar (a press pinned the dock and never scrolled), a left-edge dock's grip owned column 0 of three activity-bar rows, and a hidden side column's grip painted over an `always` dock's own item glyphs while their hits still fired. **One rule, not three patches: an edge band belongs to one surface, and a grip only ever takes cells its own surface's band owns.** A side dock's band is reserved by layout (see the dock's row above); a side column's band is the outermost column of `frameRects(…).upper` — the editor area, with the dock's band, the bottom panel, the bar, the statusline and the `:` line already taken off it — rather than the whole screen column, which used to run through both the dock's glyphs and an open bottom panel's header chips. `place` centres the run on both axes, so the glyph sits in the middle column of a three-wide band, where the strip's own items paint theirs. The bottom edge keeps the one band mnml shares on purpose: the grip stands on the `:` line's row and `gripBlocked` stands it down while a line is open. A grip may still stand on a pane's own CONTENT — the surface it summons covers that text a moment later — never on a control. `src/app/edge_band_audit.zig` is the structural guard: every dock edge × mode × strip state × both columns × the menu bar × the right column × the bottom panel × the activity bar, at 80×24, 120×40 and 376×92, rendered for real and asked whether any grip took another surface's cell and whether any scrollbar, activity-bar row or dock item is shadowed. `tests/e2e/edge_grip_side_{dock_rail,dock_scrollbar,sidebar_over_dock,sweep}.test` |

| Filter the box: `/` (Ctrl+F in the standard profile) | done | `Filter` / `filterKey` / `filtered` / `rowMatches` / `drawFilter` in `src/ui/settings.zig`; `lists` / `refocus` / `paste` and the `.filter` hit prong in `src/app/settings.zig`; `tests/e2e/settings_filter.test` | Rust has this (`SettingItem::matches_row` / `filter_settings` / `settings_filter_focus` in `src/app/settings.rs`, row 0 of the inner area in `src/ui/settings_overlay.rs`). `// changed:` five ways, all listed in `docs/BACKPORT.md` §12. (1) The field is a `text_field`, not Rust's append-only `s.filter.push(c)` with a painted `▏` — caret, `←→`, Home/End, word deletes and paste from day one. (2) The match reads the label, the word the **current** value reads as (`always`, `on`, a number row's digits) and the **section's name**; Rust reads the label and **every option a row offers**, so its `always` finds rows that merely could be. (3) The pill sits under the title and **above the section strip** (Zig-only chrome), which stays and dims the sections with no match — its click still jumps, by name, since the visible list has lost their headers. (4) The footer swaps `22/92` for `5 of 87`, and a dead query says `no setting matches "…"` where the rows would be. (5) Rust focuses the field on overlay open (a vscode-keyboard finding); Zig's list binds `j` `k` `h` `l` `r` `R` `g` `G` `q`, so `/` opens it instead — Ctrl+F too in the standard profile. The cursor is clamped to a match on every query change (a still-matching row keeps the focus), `↑↓` walk matches from the field, Enter hands the list back with the query on, `←→` then adjusts, and Esc clears the query before it cancels the box. The hint row gained `/ search` (a fifth form in `hintFor`, which drops `↑↓ move` before the search key). One command id, `view.settings_search` — the row below |
| `view.settings_search` — the filter from the palette | **Zig-only** | `openSearch` in `src/app/settings.zig`, `openSettingsSearch` in `src/app/cmd_view.zig`; `tests/e2e/settings_filter.test` | `/` is only discoverable once the box is open, so `ctrl+shift+p` → `Settings: search…` opens it with the pill already holding the keys. No chord of its own — the box's `/` (and Ctrl+F in the standard profile) is that. `docs/BACKPORT.md` §12 |
| Settings → Editor → Always show fold arrows | done | the `ui.always_show_fold_arrows` row in `settings.zig`, read by `editor_view.zig` | the gutter's chevron is otherwise offered only under the pointer, so nothing says the gutter folds; written to the home config. A `ui.` key in the Editor section — it is the editor's own gutter |
| Right-panel visible / width rows | done | the number rows in `settings.zig`, `ui.right_panel_visible` / `_width` | |
| Startup picker overlay | done | `src/app/startup_picker.zig` | `// changed:` a workspace row names the relaunch |
| `MNML_STARTUP_PICKER=1` | done | `wanted` in `startup_picker.zig` | also when the workspace is `$HOME` |
| `--startup-picker` flag | done | `src/main.zig` | sets `MNML_STARTUP_PICKER=1` for the process |
| `mnml.app` launcher default | done | `dist/macos/{Info.plist,launcher.sh,build-app.sh}`, `scripts/package.sh --macos-app` | ghostty first, Terminal.app else; the picker on |
| Update check on launch | done | `src/app/update.zig` | |
| `ui.check_updates = false` opt-out | done | `Config.zig`, `update.zig` | + `MNML_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1` |
| Skipped in headless | done | the `startup` hook is the terminal loop's | |
| `zig build docs` → `docs/commands.md` | done | `tools/gen_commands.zig`, `build.zig` | Zig-only |
| `zig build check` (E7 gates) | done | `build.zig`, `tools/break-check.sh`, `tests/e2e/defaults.test` | fmt → Debug tests → ReleaseSafe tests → the gate → the width sweep → `defaults.test` → the full corpus; Zig-only |
| Session file `.mnml/session.zon` | done | `src/app/session.zig` | ZON, never JSON |
| Lua scripting — `.mnml/init.lua`, the `mnml` table | done | `src/scripting/lua.zig`, `src/scripting/api.zig`, `script.reload` / `script.edit_init`, the `init_lua` trust sink | beyond the Rust list (D10); a 20 ms budget per entry. *2026-09-23 (luafix):* a trip cannot be caught — the `pcall` / `xpcall` scripts see rethrow it — and the pattern matcher checks the budget inside one `string.find` (a patched `lstrlib.c` under `vendor/lua54/`); `setmetatable` refuses a raw `__gc`, since a finalizer runs with hooks off where the budget cannot reach; `load` compiles source text only (mode `"t"`); a statusline segment that errors toasts once and is not polled again until a reload |
| Lua decorations + the diagnostics sink — `mnml.decor.*`, `mnml.diagnostics.*` | done | `src/app/script_decor.zig`, the `mnml.decor` / `mnml.diagnostics` blocks of `src/scripting/api.zig`, `applyLuaDiagnostics` in `src/app/lsp.zig`, `LineGround` / `VirtualLine.below` / `GutterMark.priority` in `src/ui/editor_view.zig` | Zig-authored — the Rust editor has no script decorations. Four decorations in a namespace the script owns (virtual text at `eol` / `above` / `below`, a gutter sign with a priority, a role over a byte range, a whole-row ground), anchored to bytes that follow every edit; a namespace goes with `script.reload`. The sink is a fourth source on the per-file diagnostics keyed by `(namespace, path)`, so the gutter, the squiggle, the statusline count, the DIAGNOSTICS panel and `]d` show a script's findings under its own `source`. `docs/LUA.md`; `tests/e2e/lua_decor.test`, `lua_diagnostics_sink.test` |
| Lua live picker source + preview column + multi-select | done | `live` / `preview` / `multi` / `on_accept` in the `mnml.picker` block of `src/scripting/api.zig`, `PreviewRow` / `placeWith` / `drawPreview` / `Outcome.toggle` in `src/ui/picker.zig`, `toggleMark` / `tick` in `src/app/cmd_picker.zig` | Zig-authored — the Rust picker has no preview column and no live source. `items(query)` is asked again as the query changes, debounced 80 ms, and the previous call's rows stay until the new ones land; `preview(row)` fills a right-hand column (results left, a `│` rule, the box widened to 120) decoded when the cursor moves, never in the paint loop; `Tab` marks a row and steps on, Enter hands `on_accept` the list. Rows gain `icon` and `data` — the row table itself comes back. `docs/LUA.md`; `tests/e2e/lua_picker_live.test`, `lua_example_recent_commands.test` |
| Lua list helper + rail sections — `mnml.list{}`, `mnml.section{}`, `mnml.pane.open{ list }` | done | `src/app/script_list.zig`, `src/app/script_section.zig`, `src/ui/script_list.zig`, `Section.script` / `RailRow` / `railOrder` in `src/ui/activity_bar.zig`, `PanelId.script` in `src/core/panel.zig` | Zig-authored — the Rust editor's sections are all built in. `mnml.list{}` is the `ListPanel` TODOS is (caps header, refresh and `sort:` chips, filter pill, `j`/`k`/`g`/`G`/Enter, fold headers, scrollbar, row menu, hits); the script answers only with rows, asked on creation, on `l:refresh()`, on the chip and on a sort change. `mnml.section{}` gives it a real activity-bar row — its own glyph, its own place from `after` — and a column of its own, in `rects.json` as `rail:script:N` / `row:script:N` / `chip:script:refresh`, all dropped on `script.reload`; `mnml.pane.open{ list = l }` is the pane form. `docs/LUA.md`; `tests/e2e/lua_section.test`, `lua_example_todo_list.test` *2026-09-22 (`zl-fixes`):* the rows fn, `on_enter` and `on_menu` are called in the state that registered them (`App.luaState`), so an INSTALLED script's list works — the shipped `todo-list` aborted the process on Install before |
| Lua text operations + operator registration — `mnml.buf.selection` / `range` / `word_at`, `mnml.operator{}`, `mnml.commands` | done | the `mnml.buf` block of `src/scripting/api.zig`, `src/input/script_ops.zig`, `PendingOp.script` / `finishOperator` in `src/input/vim.zig`, `AppCommand.script_operator`, `runOperatorMode` | Zig-authored. A script reads a range (`selection` with its `char` / `line` / `block` shape, `range`, `word_at`) and registers an operator that reaches both profiles by their own road: under vim the `g<letter>` chord goes into a table the handler asks once its own `g` switch has fallen through, so `gs{motion}`, `gsiw`, `3gsw`, `gss` and `V…gs` all build the range the way `gU{motion}` does; under standard the chord is an ordinary `user.<id>` command taking the selection, or the word under the cursor. Whatever `run` applies is one undo step. `docs/LUA.md`; `tests/e2e/lua_operator.test`, `lua_example_surround_word.test` |
| Lua `cursor_idle` hook; `mnml.task.run{ hidden, on_line }` | done | `src/app/idle.zig`, `src/app/script_task.zig`, `Hook.cursor_idle` in `src/core/hooks.zig` | Zig-authored. `cursor_idle` fires 300 ms after the cursor stops, once per resting place; `buffer_change`, documented since D10 and emitted nowhere, fires too — debounced per editor pane and naming the pane that was edited, never on a focus switch (luafix, 2026-09-23). A `hidden` task has no pane and streams its output to `on_line` a line at a time (stderr merged in, 5000 lines of 4 KiB capped). The two shipped examples are the acceptance: `lua/{git-blame-line,eslint}/init.lua`, `tests/e2e/lua_example_git_blame_line.test`, `lua_example_eslint.test` |
| Installed scripts — a script is a directory, one Lua state each, `installed · marketplace · dev` | done | `src/scripting/manifest.zig`, `src/app/scripts.zig`, `src/app/scripts_panel.zig`, `src/app/script_doctor.zig`, `Lua.createFor` / `requireFn` in `src/scripting/lua.zig`, `LuaRef.state` in `src/core/command.zig`, sink `script_install` in `src/config/trust.zig` | Zig-authored — the Rust editor has no plugin install path at all. A script is a directory with a `script.zon` (`name`, `version`, `api = 1`, the commands and hooks it claims, where it came from); each installed one gets its OWN Lua state — its own budget clock, its own decoration namespaces, its own registrations, and a `require` that resolves only under its own directory — so one erroring is one toast and a disabled row. `script.install` takes a git URL (shallow clone), an archive or a folder, stages it, and puts the manifest's claims plus a grep for `task.run` in the trust dialog BEFORE the first run. The SCRIPTS section gains the INTEGRATIONS section's three tabs, painted by the same `drawSection`. `script.doctor` is the receipt. `docs/LUA.md`; `tests/e2e/scripts_install_dir.test`, `scripts_marketplace_default.test`, `scripts_marketplace_local.test`, `scripts_trust_claims.test`, `scripts_dev_root_reload.test`, `scripts_doctor.test` |
| Lua platform polish — `mnml.inspect`, the argument-error contract, the budget chip, the doc check | done | `inspect` / `writeInspect` and the `needStr` / `needFn` / `needTable` / `argStr` / `argInt` helpers in `src/scripting/api.zig`, `src/scripting/doc_check.zig`, `Entry.budget_hits` / `budget_glyph` in `src/ui/integrations_view.zig`, `writeSources` in `src/app/script_doctor.zig` | Zig-authored — §5 of `docs/research/lua-platform-design-2026-09-13.md`. `mnml.inspect(v)` writes any value out for reading (tables four deep, keys sorted so it is deterministic, `<cycle>` for a table that names itself); `print` stays the log. Every `mnml.*` argument check names the CALL, the ARGUMENT and the SHAPE (`mnml.picker.source: `items` must be a function(query) returning a table of rows`) and lands before the first allocation — a Lua error is a longjmp, so an `errdefer` under one never runs. A script that trips the 20 ms budget wears a `⏱ N` chip on its SCRIPTS row, painted at the right edge so it survives the shipped `ui.tree_width = 30`; `script.doctor` gains the three scan roots and each script's `require` root. The `mnml.` completion popup lists the sub-tables before the root functions. `docs/LUA.md` is a reference with one `####` heading per function, walked against the registry in both directions by `doc_check.zig`. `tests/e2e/lua_inspect.test`, `scripts_budget_chip.test`, `lua_completion.test` |
| Bridge v2 — `Pane.mount` over a socket | done | `src/bridge/host.zig` / `wire.zig`, `src/app/mount_pane.zig`, `mount.open` | beyond the Rust list; the SDK is `sdk/mnml-sdk` |
